MINISTRY OF COAL 

New Delhi, the 22nd September, 2008 

S.O. 2692—Whereas, by the notification of the 
Government of India in the Ministry of Coal, number 
S. O. 1625 dated the 28th April, 2006, issued under sub¬ 
section (l) of Section 7 of the Coal Bearing Areas 
(Acquisition and Development) Act, 1957 (20 of 1957), 
(hereinafter referred to as the said Act) and published in 
the Gazette of India, Part-ll, Section-3 sub-section (ii), dated 
the 29th April, 2006, the Central Government gave notice 
of its intention to acquire the lands and all rights in or over 
such lands in the locality specified in the Schedule 
appended to that notification; 

And, whereas, the competent authority in pursuance 
of Section 8 of the said Act, has made his report to (he 
Central Govern ment; 


And, whereas, the Central Government after 
considering the report aforesaid and after consulting the 
Government of Maharashtra, is satisfied that the lands 
measuring 254,15 hectares (approximately) or 62B.03 acres 
(approximately) and all rights in or over such lands as 
described in die Schedule appended hereto, should be 
acquired; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred 
by sub-section (I) of Section 9 of the Cod Bearing Areas 
(Acquisition and Development) Act, 1957(20of 1957), the 
Central Government hereby declares that the land 
measuring 254,15 hectares (approximately) or 628.03 acres 
(approximately) and all rights in or over such lands as 
described in the Schedule are hereby acquired. 

The plan bearing number C-l (E)/l 11/JIM R/760- 0508 
dated the 21st May, 2008 of the area covered by this 
notification, may be inspected in the office of the Collector, 
Chandrapur (Maharashtra) or in the office of the Coal 
Controller, t, Council House Street, Kolkata (Pin 700 001) 
or in the office of the Western Coalfields Limited (Revenue 
Department), Coal Estate, Civil Lines, Nagpur - 440 001 
(Maharashtra).

S^O- 265S*—whereas, it appears to the Central 
Government that Coal is likety to be obtained from the 
lands in the locality mentioned in the Schedule hereto 
annexed; 

Now t therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred 
by sub-section (1) of Section 4 of the Coal Bearing Areas 
(Acquisitions and Development) Act, 3957 (20 of 1957) 
(hereinafter referred to as the said Act), the Centra) 
Government hereby gives notice of its intention to prospect 
for coal therein; 

The p]an bearing number DG/8735 dated the 3rd Jn ly n 
2Q0E of the area covered by this notification can be inspected 
at die office of Chief General Manager (Exploration Division), 
Central Mine Planning and Design Institute, Gondwana 
Place, Kanke Road, Ranchi or at the office of the Coal 
Controller, 1, Council House Street, Kolkolaor at the office 
of the District Collector, Raigarb, ChattiEgath. 

All persons interested in the land covered by this 
notification shall deliver all mops, charts and other 
documents referred in sub-section (7) of Section 13 of the 
said Act to the Chief General Manager (Exploration 
Division), Central Mine Planning and Design Institute, 
Gondwana Place, Kanke Road, Ranchi within ninety days 
from ihe date of publication of this notification.

S. O. 2710^ Whereas, it appears to the Central Government, that it is 
necessary in the public interest that for the transportation of petroleum products from 
Devanagondhi to New Bangalore International Airport Devanahaili in the Stale of 
Karnataka, a pipeline should be laid by the Indian Oil Corporation Limited. 


And, whereas, it appears that for the purpose of laying such pipeline, it is necessary 
to acquire the right of user in the land described in the schedule annexed to this 
notification; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (1) of section 3 of 
the Petroleum and Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in Land) Act, 
1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby declares its intention to acquire 
the right of user therein; 


Any person interested in the land described in the said Schedule may, within twenty 
one days from the date on which the copies of this notification, as published in the 
Gazette of India, are made available to the general public, object in writing to the 
acquisition of the right of user therein or laying of the pipeline under the land, to

AWARD 

This Industrial Dispute between the above said 
parties was referred by the Desk Officer, Government of 
India. Ministry of Labour, New Delhi vide order No. 

- L-42012/181 /2Q05-1R (CM-II) dated 14-09-2006 to the Central 
Government Industrial Tribunal-cum-Labour Court, 
Ahmedabad for adjudication under Section 10 (I) of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947- The said dispute was 


subsequently transferred to this Tribunal by order of (he 
competent authority. The dispute between the panties 
relates to the following demands of (ha wortsmen concerned. 
‘‘Whether the action of the management of Central 
Public Works Department, Gandhinagar in 
terminating the services of Sh, Vijay Haribhai Pamnar 
and 5 others from the date of their respective 
termination as per ^enclosed, is legal and justified ? 
If not, t>o what relief the concerned workmen are 
entitled T 

After the case was transferred to this Tribunal, the 
unices were sent to the parties. It appeals that there are in 
all six workmen concerned in this case. The main workman 
is Vijay Haribhai Parmar of whose address is given in the 
order of Reference. Said Vijay Haribhai Parmar was served 
as per Ex. 5, ho we ver, neither he nor h is fellow workmen are 
attending This Tribunal, It therefore appears that none of 
the workmen have any interest in this case. This Reference 
case is, therefore, dismissed for want of prosecution. Order 
accordingly with no order as to costs.

5 1 Considering the deposition of the workman, the 
days of his service does not seem to be challenged by the 
employer in the cross examination. Similarly p his allegation 
that KM Satyad and KG Adhyani who were taken after him 
were continued in the service is also not challenged in the 
cross examination. The only material admission that was 
obtained in the cross examination was that he was given 
worlc when the permanent *D 7 staff proceeds on leave. He 
has denied that he had not worked continuously, 

6. The employer examined Shri Panabhai 
Chhaganbhai Gamit as its witness at Ex. 37 who is Deputy 
Post Master at Vadodara. He admits that the workman was 
working on daily wages basis and was given work when 
permanent employee proceeds on leave; that he was paid 
wages for the days he had worked; that he was given work 
with a view that the work of postal department may not be 
stopped in the absence of the permanent employee. It is 
staled by the witness that he was not appointed as per 
rules of the Government. In the cross examination, the 
witness was shown Ex. 20 which Ls the details of presence 
of the workman produced by the employer vide list Ex. 14. 
The witness states that the muster roll and pay roll of the 
workman are in the custody of the accounts office. He 
states that the Post Master can retain outsider on work; 
lhat in 1986 the qualification for Group b D' was seventh 
standard pass. The witness admits that the work which 
was taken from the workman was that of a permanent 
employee in the later’s absence. Rest of the cross 
examination does not seem to be material for the purpose 
of this case. 

7. tf we consider the deposition of the witness of the 
employer, only one fact is coming out and that is - the 
workman was given work when the permanent employee 
proceeds on leave. It appears that at the relevant time the 
workman being SSC failed, was having qualification for 
being appointed as Group ‘D 7 stiff¬ 
ly The first closing pursish of workman is at Ex. 28 

and second is at Ex. 60. Similarly, the employer has also 
given two closing pmsish vide Ex. 38 and 65, The written 
arguments of the workman is at Ex. 66 and that of the 
employer is at Ex. 76.1 have considered the rival contentions 
of the parties in the written arguments together with the 
evidence on record. 

9. The undisputed fact is that the workman had 
entered the service of the employer on 13-08-1986 and had 
worked Up to 02-09-19B8. Barring August and September, 
1986 and the last month September, I98S, the workman 


had put in 23 to 30 days work in each month. Indisputably, 
the workman had put in more than 294 days of service 
whhin twelve months prior to his termination. Admittedly, 
he was not given notice of retrenchment* nor retrenchment 
compensation was offered or given to him. The only defence 
of the employer as is appear from the record is that the 
workman was given work because the permanent Group 
L D b employee had proceeded on leave and also with a view 
to see that the work of the post office should noi be 
stopped. If we go through Sec. 25F of the Industrial Disputes 
Act, wherein conditions precedent to retrenchment of 
workmen is stated* we do not find a provision thai if a 
workman is retained to do work of a permanent employee 
continuously for more than two years, he can be discharged 
without payment of retrenchment of compensation etc. 
Therefore, in the humble opinion of this Tribunal, the so 
called termination of the workman on 02-09-198 8 was 
retrenchment of the workman and since lhe conditions 
precedent were not followed by the employer, the said 
retrenchment was in violation of the provisions of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, In the written argumenis of the 
employer it is contended that the workman cannot be 
considered to be permanent because his appointment was 
not done after written test, oral interview etc. Sec. 251-' of 
the Industrial Disputes Act also does put a condition lhat 
the workman concerned should be a permanent workman 
and should have been appointed after written test, oral 
interview etc. The contention in the written arguments of 
the employer that the workman had not completed 240 days 
in any of the year is devoid of any merits because it is 
against the record produced in this case, 

10. From Ex. 20 it also does not appear that the 
workman was given work only when the permanent 
employee proceeds on leave h On several occasions the 
workman was given work on vacant post which fell vacant 
on account of retirement of concerned employee. On several 
occasion the workman was given work because the holder 
of the said post was sent on deputation to somewhere 
else. Thus, the workman had rightly contended in ihc 
statement of claim as also in his deposition that he was 
given work on vacant post. 

11. The learned Assistant Government Pleader drew 
my attention to the written arguments of the employer and 
submitted that postal department is not an industry within 
the definition of the “industry” given in the Industrial 
Disputes Acl, 1947 r He also cited decision of Hon. Supreme 
Court in Sub-Divisional Inspector of Post, Vaikam and 
Others Versus Theyyain Joseph etc. which was published 
in 1996II LLJ 230.1 have gone through the said decision, 11 
was held by Hon. Supreme Court that to provide 
telecommunication service to the general public and 
amenity, was essential pan of the sovereign function of 
the welfare state and hence the postal department was not 
an industry as defined in the Industrial Disputes Act, The 
learned AGP has also produced Swamy's Service Rules lor

ORDER 

This Reference care is allowed. The action of the 
concerned Postal Authorities as slated in the schedule to 
the order of Reference in terminating the services of Shri 
G S. Dudhwala is held to be unjustified. The workman is 
held entitled to reinstatement with continuity of service 
but without back wages. The said Postal Authorities shall 
reinstate the said workman within one month from the date 
of publication of this award. 

There is no order as to costs.

AWARD 
Passed on 12-S-OS 

Central Government vide notification no. L-420 1 ] / 
16/9l-lR(D,U0 dated 8-10-91, referred the following 
industrial dispute for judicial adjudication to this 
Tribunal : — 

" Weather the action of the Chief Engineer, Bhakra 
Dam* Nangal Township in declining to 

give pay protection to the workmen (as peT annexure) 
is justified? If not, to what relief the concerned 
workmen are entitled? 7 ' 

As per the pleadings of the panics, all of the workmen 
named in the annexure list to the reference, which are 18 in 
No. are retrenches of Beas Dam) BSL project. Out of the 
above workmen, the workmen mentioned at serial No. 1-11 
were re-employed in B.R.S. division whereas,the workmen 
mentioned from serial No, 12-17 were re-employed in the 
Bhakra Mechanical Division and the last workmen 
rnentioned al serial no 18 was re-employed in the Bhakra 
Dam, Division Nangal by the Chief Engineer* Bhakra Dam. 
It was, the fresh appointment of the workmen and the benefit 
vide letter No. 5259-61/BMB/1713/46/SUB, daicd 13-5-76 
regarding the increments to retrenched employees was 
given to all the workmen at the time of their fresh 
appointment. All the workmen accepted the benefit of 
increments and after their appointment raised the industrial 
dispute for the protection of the salary of basic pay which 
they were getting at the time of their retrenchment from 
previous assignment. 

All the workmen filed the affidavits and they were 
cross-examined by the Learned legal advisor of the 
management of B.B.M.B. Almost all the workmen have 


54?j 

accepted that for ihc reappointment of the retrenches there 
is a scheme for awarding the increment and their pay had 
been fixed in accordance with the, said policy Die main 
question for the determination of this Tribunal is whclher 
the workman is entitled for their protection of pay on fresh 
appointment to the B.B.M.B by the Chief-Engineer? 

It Is true that all the employees went fixed to the 
lesser basic pay as compared from the basic pay they were 
geuing in their previous assignment ai the time of ihcir 
retrenchment. But as stated earlier that their appointment 
i$ fresh in the different capacity artd as per the policy of tlu: 
B.B.M.B., which is accepted by all the workmen, they were 
given the benefit of the fixation nf pay by giving some 
additional increments on the length of service. As per the 
letter dated 13-5-76, which is the also decision of ihc 
B.B.M.B. for every retrenches on his re-appointment one 
increment will be given for two years services in the 
department from which he was retrenched subject io the 
five maximum increments. As per this policy decision, the 
pay of all the workmen was fixed and accepted by the 
workmen. Now, it is not open to them to re-open Lhe matter 
once again for fixation of the salary on die basis of the 
basic pay scale they were drawing In the previous 
department at the time of their reircnchmcnt. I he 
appointment of all the workmen is fresh. They have been 
given the benefit of the increments as per the above policy 
of B.B.M.B. and all the workmen have received the 
retrenchment compensation from the department, they were 
retrenched. Accordingly, this reference is answered 
positively that ihe action of the Chief Engineer Bhakra 
Dam, B.B,M.R., Nangal Township* 1 in declining to give 
pay protection lo the workmen (as per annexure) is justified 
and the workmen, are not entitled to any relief Lot the 
Central Government be informed. Pile is consigned. 

G, K. SHARMA, Presiding Officer

Learned counsel lor the management liiis (ik'd the 
written arguments as well. Opportunity fbroral arguments 
was given to the parlies. The main uontentiun of learned 
council far the management is that when the workman sought 
the regularization of the service he was over-aged. Tliun¬ 
being over-aged his ease was nnl rightly considered and 
accordingly he was terminated from the service. I.earned 
counsel for the mmiygemuni has retied upon a case law 




t*TFT]I-^I«5 3(ii)] 


TT fMHi : fttoHN 27^ SOOW^nR^f 5 ± 19J0 


S425 


published in 2007(2} Supreme Q»t Cases (L&S) 534 Trimail 
Triupeti Dcmthamms Vs K. iyothcswam Pillai and 
another. This b the law on the relaxation of the age and 
learned counsel for the managemwt has rightly sakl that in 
para no^ 7 of the claim statement Shri Subey Singh has 
mentioned that he was over-aged and he cannot sought 
regutarration of hb service In this regard, 1 will prefer to 
mention that he question far adjudication before thfe Tribunal 
b not regarding 1l>e regularization of the services of the 
wovkwnbut to bold vrtiettohis terrriHiBtionWndiesetvm 
is legal and justified There is no iota of evidence on record 
that at thetime otitis initial appointment to Jawahar Nsvcriaya 
Vidpalaya, the workman was over-aged and his initial 
appointment was noi valid. Accordingly the case law 
referred by learned counsel is not applicable in the instant 
case. 

Learned counsel for the management has also relied 
upotta case law published in 200S{ 1) Supreme Court Cases 
683 Additional Manager, AravuliGolf Club and another Vs 
Chandarhau and another, which is on creation of post. It 
has been held by the Hon 1 ble the Apex Court than creation of 
and sanction of posts is the prerogative to the Legislative 
Authority and not of the Courts, Agpih the question before 
this Tribunal is not for creation of posts but forthe justification 
of the termination of the workman. Accordingly* this case 
law is not applicable in the instant case. The another case law 
relied upon by the learned counsel of the management is Bala 
Krishna Behera and another Vs. Satya Patkash Dass, which 
is on the power ofthe High Court by the writ of mandamus to 
direct the appointment of a person against the post which 
has been abolished. Again the question before this Tribunal 
is regarding the justification ofthe termination order and the 
case law referred by the learned counsel for the management 
is not applicable Tn Krishana Bbagya Ja! Nigam Limited Vs. 
Mohamad Rah 2007( 1) £CC (L£S}679 HotTble Supreme Cotrt 
has held that the burden of proof lies on the workman to 
prove that he has work continuously for 240 days in the 
preceding year from the date of his termination and he was 
paid the wages for 240 days he worked. 

Accordingly, the workman has to prove that he has 
worked for 240 days continuously in the pieced ingyear front 
the date of his termination and he was paid wages for that 
period. 

From the pleadings ofthe parties and the affidavit they 
filed, it is clear that the workman Shri Subey Singh was 
appointed vide Ex. W.2 on 12-4-91 on daily wages at the rates 
prescribed by Deputy Commissioner w.e.f. 12-4-91 and the 
wages were to be paid for the days of duty only and not on 
the monthly basis. The wages were paid on ihe clays he 
worked is explained by the FTinciapI, Navodaya Vidyalaya in 
his cross-examination on 1 3-9-2006 that the paymenl to the 
workman used to be given after completion of month. Ihus* 
as per the evidence of Shri Ompa] Thonw, Principal Inchttge 
of Jawahar Navodya Vidya]aya and the appointment loiter 


dated 12-4-91, the uK*k£rt» was paid forthe days be wmked 
but the mod* ofpayment was monthly. FremEx£.4*5*6*7£ 
t which are the original tetters filed by the workman, it is 
clear that the workmanworked from 124-91 to 30-6-92, He 
was again reappointed on 1*7-92 and worked iqs to 13-8-92. 
Meaning thereby, there was no break in his subsequent 
appointment. TTiereafter, he was further re-appointed on 
14*8-92; though he resumed duty on 17-R-92 and worked up 
to 15-12*92, when hewasrelfevedfiBinthe duties by Principal 
Jawahar Navodaya Vidyatoya* Dtvrala, vide letter dated 
1M2-92 Ex. W2. 

There is one more contention of learned counsel for 
the management that the M.W. 1 has ttf^jorically stated in 
his evidence that in summer and winter breaks Shri Subey 
Singh had not worked, 1 am unable to accept this contention 
of the management because the vary letters of the 
management which are filed by the workman shows that the 
wodaviHnwaswtukingvimidl>oernberandon 19-6-92. Ex. 
W7 is the relieving order given by the Principal Jawahar 
Navodaya Vidyabyo, Devrala containing tile contents that 
Shri Subey Singh was engaged on daily wages in this 
Vidyalaya on 12-4-91 (FN.) and was relieved w,e£ 30^92 
(A.N). Month of June is the period of summer vacations on 
which as per the relieving order given by the management, 
the workman has worked. 

It is true that workman has to prove that he had worked 
continuously for 240 days preceding the date of his 
terminating and was paid the wages for 240 days accordingly. 
The statement in the statement of claim and tn affidavit will 
not be sufficient but the workman has to prove the above 
facts by some cogent evidence. The workman accordingly 
has filed the original documents to prove this fact. 

From the perusal of the record, it is established that 
initial appointment of the workman was proper, It has not 
been challenged by the management that ihe initial 
appointment of the workman was not regular. He worked 
conlttiLJOUsly from 3 4-4*91 to 30-6-92 except three days breaks 
of 14-8-92,15-8-92 and 16-8-92, Accordingly the total working 
days comes to 480 days approximately, As per the evidence 
ofthe management the workman was paid monthly but on 
the basis of dates he worked. Accordingly, as per the policy 
Sundays and Holidays are to be counted while calculating 
the working days. The workman was released on 30-6-92 and 
preceding to the year from the date of his terminal ion. he has 
completed 240 days continuously and was paid there nlT. 

He was retrenched wilhout ary notice orieircnchmcinl 
compensation, Ihus, his termination is illegal in the eyes of 
law, 

Now the question is what relief should be given to the 
workman? As per the statement ofthe witness of the 
management, the workman was relieved from the service 
because, ibere was no vacancy. There were two vacancy* 
for the casual labour which were taken away by tin: 






5426 


[Part II—Sncr. 3(H)} 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA:SEPTEMBER27,2008/ASVINA5« 1950 


Government Thus, the direction forTOnstmement into service 
will not be possible as there is no post and as per the law [aid 
down by the Hon'ble Apex Court la Divisional Manager, 
Aravali Golf Chib and another Es. Chandarhass and another 
(dupna) it is not within the preview of the Tribunal to direct For 
the creation of die post* In such the ease*, the Tribunal 
should award the compensation on reasonable eases and 
this Tribunal is authorized to do so. The reasonable bases for 
awarding the compensalion includes the amount of 
tubendunent compensation which would have been given 
by the management to the workman at the time ofhis relieving 
from the services, depreciation in money from the date of 
removal from the service till the payment oi"compensation, 
interest on the amount and the litigation expenses incurred 
by the workman For the wrongful act of the management. 
Considering the above facts as the workman has stated in his 
cross-examination that at the time of his removal from the 
service he used to get Rs. S35 p.m. and considering all the 
facts mentioned above* Rs. 25,000 will be the appropriate 
compensation to be given to (he workman. Accordingly, this 
reference is answered in negative That the action of the 
management of J&wahar Navcdaya Vidyalaya. Devrala in 
terminating the services of Shri Subcy Singh ex'gnotip D daily 
paid employee w.e.f 15-12-1992 h not just Fair and legal. I he 
workman is entitled for the compensation of Rs 25,000 from 
the management. Management is accordingly directed to pa> 
Rs 25.000 within a month. Let the Central Government be 
informed. File is consigned. 

G. K. SHARMA, Presiding Officer

2 + The case of second party in brief is that be was 
employed in telephone exchange at Nandgaon since 1998- 
Hb job was to rectify telephone and cable faults, dig

time, he inhumed that the J TO was hiding impressed 


[Part U—Skc. 3(ii)J 


account but has deposed that the contract labours were 
paid by the respect tve coni factors. 

He ad mined in the cross-examination that some 
casual workers were regularised in Daund Division by the 
First Party. He could \m eerily by which mode those casual 
labours were regularised. Any documentary evidence has 
also noi been adduced on behalf oh he First Party. 

15. The learned Advocate—Mr. A.Y. Shikarkhanc, 
for the Second Party, has subtniced that the Second Party 
has completed continuous 240 days in the emloyment of 
the First Party-. But at the same time, while terminating his 
services, the compliance of Section 25-F was not done by 
the First Party by not paying any retrenchment 
compensation and hence, he be reinstated with continuity 
of service and full back wages. 

Per contra, the learned Advocate—Mr. Zende, for 
the First Party, has submitted that the entire burden was 
on the Second Party to prove that he had worked with the 
l-'irst Party but for wans of tiny documentary evidence, it 
could not be proved.! le picked the admission given by the 
witness No, 2 in cross examination that he was the only 
employee working at Nangaon. He also submitted that JTQ 
—Tlwart had not been examined by the Second Party 
though he is still in the employment of the First Party. VTe 
placed his reliance on the cases of Mukund Staffs Officers 
Association v/s. Mukund Limited [2007 (6) ALL MR 312] 
and Secretary* State of Karnataka and others v/s. Umadevi 
and others (AIR 2006 Supreme Court 1806). 

16.I laving considered the submission tabled by both 
the sides, considering the ratio of the authority of Mukund 
Staff Officers Asocial!on v/s. Mukund L imited [2007 (6) 
ALL MR 312], it is clem that ihe burden is on the Second 
Party to establish it when the claim has been denied by the 
First Party. The sum and substance of the second authority 
relied upon by the Firs-t Party Is That the temporary 
employees appointed in violation of the constitutional 
scheme do not have any legal right to be permanently 
absorbed in the eniploymcnl.The facts of the instant case 
being absolutely different than thai of the foregoing 
authority, itcannor be made applicable in the instant case 
because it has been nowhere taken ihe stand by the First 
Party about the appointment by violation of the 
constiiutional scheme. 

!7. Having considered the foregoing discussions 
coupled with the corroborated testimony of Ihe Second 
Party and failure on the pun of the first party to adduce 
any adverse evidence, l daimtlessly reach the conclusion 
lhal the employer-cmployee relationship exists between 
the Second Party and FArst Party- and as the compliance of 
Section 25-F was noi done by the First Party at the Lime of 
termination of services, it is held thai he was illegally 
terminated by the l : irsr Party. I therefore, answer this Issue 
Nos. 1 and 2 accorditigly. 

18. Issue Nu. 3: In view of the findings on the issue 
Nos. 1 and 2, keping in view ihe facts and circumstances of 
the Second Part) 1 witness, so far as the retrenchment and 
continuity of service arc concerned,, the Second Party is 








:’feW7 27 P 2008/sqrftTT5 J 1930 


5441 


veiy much entitled to the reliefs claimed in that regard. 
However, in the entire evidence, as it has not been brought 
on record by the Second Party about his efforts to acquire 
any alternative gainful employment, there being no other 
option, the Second Party i$ not at all emitted to the relief of 
back wages as claimed, J, therefore, answer this issue No, 3 
accordingly, and in the aftermath, pass following order: 

ORDER 

The reference is allowed* The First Party is directed 
to reinslate the Second Party with continuity of service as 
a foil time casual labour in regular vacancy wherever it is 
available. The prayer of back wages is rejected. No order 
as to the cost.
